pr: 878
tier: fix
offered:
closed:
---
The kernel fetched a third party's price table (the public LiteLLM list on raw.githubusercontent.com, no credential) whenever the cache was older than six hours at a build of the Token usage view's payload (`/analytics`), on an open of the view or a period picked in it, with no off switch and no trace: a table served from the built-in defaults read as live, and a failed fetch was swallowed by a bare return. ROMP_PRICE_FEED=off copies the model catalog's switch (ROMP_MODEL_CATALOG=off: the same stripped, case-folded read, placed as the first statement of _refresh_remote_prices, the one function a fetch can start in, before the TTL check and the stamp) and goes one step past the catalog's silent off: the state is said where the user looks. One function, _price_feed_status, computes the block the /analytics payload carries as priceFeed (outside the 15 s memo on both roads, and describing the table that priced the figures beside it: a landing that changes the rows clears the memo, so the click after it prices from the landed table instead of serving the old table's dollars under a block that reads live, and a build whose fetch landed during its sessions walk, after its pricing merge, prices once more from the table now) and /version carries beside modelCatalog, so the modal, /version and a curl cannot disagree. The worker publishes a landing (the cache and the fields that describe it) under _price_feed_lock, the lock the status reads its one snapshot under, so no read pairs one fetch's rows with another's counts.

What the user sees, on one muted line under the modal's footnote, worded by gear.js raPriceNote from the payload's block: with the feed live, `prices: live feed, fetched 4 minutes ago` (the age in words from the kernel's ageS through the webview's one age helper, agoWords in ui/webview/api-health-merge.ts, bound in gear.js as raAgo: now under 45 seconds, then rounded minutes, rounded hours under a day, then days); when the feed priced some of the table's models and not all (`rows` below `known`, the ids the built-in table holds), `prices: live feed for 1 of 6 models, fetched now; built-in defaults for the rest`, and when rows the feed had for known models could not be read (`matched` above `rows`) `; the feed's rows for 1 known model could not be read` follows the share; with the switch on, `prices: built-in defaults; live feed off (ROMP_PRICE_FEED=off)`; after a failed fetch, `prices: built-in defaults; the feed could not be fetched (HTTPError: HTTP 500)` (a reason class, never the response body or the URL: the exception type, an HTTP status, a wrapped socket error's type and errno, an SSL failure by OpenSSL's reason token and the verify code's entry in the kernel's copy of OpenSSL 3.0's verify-error table, such as `SSLCertVerificationError: CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED (self-signed certificate)`, `(hostname mismatch)` or `(format error in certificate's notBefore field)`, the verify message itself never read because CPython composes it with the server hostname for a hostname or IP address mismatch, and a code the table lacks, or any code under an OpenSSL before 3, reading `(verify code N)`, so the label never carries the URL or its host, a resolver failure by the EAI_ constant's name and libc's text such as `gaierror: EAI_NONAME (Name or service not known)`, never a system errno's text for a library code); on the modal's first open, `prices: built-in defaults; the feed was still being fetched when these figures were priced; pick a period to reprice` (the payload is built before the fetch it starts lands, and the next open or a period pick reads the feed); before any attempt, `prices: built-in defaults; nothing fetched from the feed yet`; after a fetch that landed and left no usable row, `prices: built-in defaults; the feed matched no known model` when no row signed to a built-in id (`matched` 0), or `prices: built-in defaults; the feed's rows for 2 known models could not be read` when rows did and none parsed. A landed or failed result outranks a fetch in flight, so the TTL re-attempt keeps saying failed or empty while its worker runs. Under off a cache already in process memory keeps serving with its age said, and the feed line then carries the refresh's own state, `prices: live feed, fetched 10 minutes ago; refresh off (ROMP_PRICE_FEED=off)`, or after a refresh that failed while the rows still serve, `; the last refresh failed (HTTPError: HTTP 500)`: the switch stops traffic, not data, and there is no cache file. Rows from the user's ~/.config/romp/model-prices.json price their models whichever table the line names, so the block counts them (`overrides`, from the merge that prices) and either line ends `; 1 row overridden by model-prices.json` when one is in effect. When ROMP_PRICE_FEED is set to a value other than off (only off turns the feed off), the feed stays on and either line ends with one more clause before the override count, `; ROMP_PRICE_FEED is set to a value that is not off, so the feed stays on (only off turns it off)`, from the block's boolean `unrecognised`, never the value. When rows of the file could not be read, either line ends, after the override count, `; 1 row of model-prices.json could not be read and was skipped (the rest of the file applies)` (`; 2 rows ... were skipped ...` above one), from the block's count `overrideRowsRejected`, never a key of the file. When the file could not be read as a JSON object at all, that last slot carries instead `; model-prices.json could not be read as a JSON object and was ignored (none of it applies)`, from the block's `overrideFault` reading `file`, never the path or the file's text; the kernel reports one class or the other, never both, so the two clauses never both render. A payload without the block (an older kernel) renders no line, and a block without a newer key (`matched`, `known`, `overrides`, `unrecognised`, `overrideFault`, `overrideRowsRejected`) is worded as the older shape. Stderr, in the catalog's idiom, each line's tail read from the same status and worded with the facts the modal's line words (the source, the share a partial feed priced, the age, the overrides), so the log and the modal cannot disagree: `price feed: off (ROMP_PRICE_FEED=off); the cost view prices tokens from the built-in defaults` once per kernel life at the first refused attempt (a kernel where nobody opens the modal says nothing; with rows already in memory the tail reads `the feed rows in memory, fetched 2 h ago` (`fetched 30 d ago` past a day, so the log counts days as the modal does), or `the feed rows in memory for 1 of 6 models, fetched 2 h ago, and the built-in defaults for the rest` when the feed priced some of the table's models, and an override in effect appends `, 1 row overridden by model-prices.json`); `price feed: fetch failed (<reason class>); ...` with the same tail, once per failed fetch, which the six-hour TTL bounds to one line per six hours; `price feed: fetch landed with no usable row (2 signed to a built-in id, none parsed); ...` once per such fetch; `price feed: fetch landed with rows for 1 known model unreadable (2 signed to a built-in id, 1 parsed); ...` once per fetch that parsed rows for some known models and not others; and `price feed: fetch failed (<reason class>), the worker thread did not start; ...` when the refresh's thread could not start, which clears the in-flight mark and records the failure instead of leaving the status reading inflight for the TTL. Three lines came with the fork manager's first review round (the round paragraph below): `price feed: ROMP_PRICE_FEED is set to '<value>', which is not off, so the feed stays on; ...` once per kernel life at the first attempt that reads such a value, the value by repr clipped to 40 characters (the kernel's own log is the one surface that carries it); and `price feed: the row 'claude-opus-4-8' in model-prices.json could not be read (not an object, a rate that is not a number, or a rate that is not finite), so that row is skipped and the rest of the file applies; ...`, once per kernel life for each skipped row, the key by repr clipped to 40 characters as the switch's value is (the manager's re-ruling of 2026-09-21; the round's line said the row and every row after it were ignored, and named no row), or `price feed: model-prices.json could not be read as a JSON object, so the file is ignored whole; ...`, once per kernel life, each fault class on a latch of its own, each with the same tail and never a value from the file or its path.

T350 kept: _model_prices(refresh=False), the spend guard's road on the pusher's path, is unchanged and never enters _refresh_remote_prices, so it cannot start a fetch with the switch off, on or unset (tests/test_spend_guard.py test_the_guard_never_starts_the_price_feed_fetch, tests/test_price_feed_off.py GuardRoad). The signature _refresh_remote_prices(now), the price-refresh thread kind and the inner def name work are unchanged, so the _THREAD_KINDS register, the stage-marks census, the perf-bench positional call and the test_token_usage.py harnesses that replace the refresh with `lambda now: None` all hold. The suite floors the switch for every test (tests/conftest.py, the model catalog floor's shape, set at import and re-asserted per test; tests/test_price_feed_floor.py pins the per-test re-assert by execution and both statements, the import-time assignment and the fixture, on conftest's source, for this switch and the catalog's; a read inside a test body cannot tell the two halves apart, and the fork manager's first review round found the import-time line unpinned), and a lab kernel built from names, which never inherits the runner's floor, is handed the switch by tests/test_ship_reship_served.py kernel_env beside the catalog's (LabKernelEnv pins it; two served labs open the Token usage view and fetched the feed on every run without it), so no test kernel reaches the feed's host; the feed's own tests pop it in setUp. The offer's tests need fork files upstream does not have, derived from each test file's imports and literal file reads at the branch head and checked against upstream's main (the text here named one until the fifth round): tests/test_price_feed_off.py imports tests/thread_ends.py; tests/test_price_feed_census.py imports tests/parse_cache.py, copies tools/file-comments-host.mjs into its scope copy (the script's declared scope names it as the one program the kernel starts from tools/) and appends a plant to hooks/romp-track-bash-guard.mjs in its walk run; tests/test_security_price_feed.py imports the census module, so it needs the same files, and reads ui/webview/link-opener.ts at import and ui/webview/url-links.ts in its new-tab anchor census; ui/webview/analytics-price-source.test.ts builds the nodes of its two executed cases, and since the fifth round ui/webview/analytics-price-source-states.test.ts those of its modal-flow case, with nodeFactory from ui/test-dom-shim.ts, so upstream's test bundle (every ui/webview/*.test.ts, through vscode-extension/esbuild.js) fails on the unresolved import; and ui/webview/chat-media-loads-browser.test.ts bundles ui/webview/md-config.ts and reads the paint attributes from ui/webview/figure-gate.ts. tests/thread_ends.py and tests/parse_cache.py ride the parked-ops liveness entry (a candidate, not yet offered); ui/test-dom-shim.ts the tags-scale-memory and shim-migration entries (fork PRs 523 and 569, both approved, neither offered yet); ui/webview/link-opener.ts and ui/webview/url-links.ts the user-todo-links entry (fork PR 405); ui/webview/md-config.ts and ui/webview/figure-gate.ts the markdown viewer's md-config entry (fork PR 707); tools/file-comments-host.mjs the file comments entry; and hooks/romp-track-bash-guard.mjs no entry. The census script needs no fork module (standard library only), but its declared scope names tools/file-comments-host.mjs and its committed counts and the table below are this tree's, so an offer regenerates scripts/network-inventory-expected.json and the table from upstream's tree. The offer therefore ports after those entries or with them; the alternative, adapting its tests for upstream (an inline join in place of thread_ends.py, a module-local parse and tree memo in place of parse_cache.py, an inline fake in place of nodeFactory, which upstream, with no ratchet, accepts, and the census's scope and plants fitted to upstream's tree), is the owner's call at offering; in the fork the ratchet in ui/test-dom-shim.test.ts requires a fake node that carries an edge (parentNode, children and the other edge names) to go through the shared module (nodeFactory, or hideEdges on the object). The settings sign-in witness, the .svg tab witness and gear.test.ts import and read nothing that upstream's tree lacks.

Design points recorded and left as they stand, each a separate decision: the share clause (`live feed for N of 6 models`) counts the table's rows the feed priced, not the models the payload's figures resolved to (sessions running one model, with a feed that priced the other five, read `live feed for 5 of 6 models` while every dollar is that model's default row), and the follow-up is a per-payload count taken where each response is priced; the catalog's own switch, ROMP_MODEL_CATALOG=off, is documented nowhere in docs/ and is silent under off (no off marker in its /version block, nothing on stderr), and giving it the same treatment is a separate change; ROMP_UPDATE_CHECK reads its switch as an exact match where the catalog strips and case-folds, the price feed copies the catalog, and the two older precedents still disagree (` Off ` counts for two switches and not the third); and the fast-mode probe and the reference's sentence that the kernel makes two API calls of its own are the same claim class as the guide's outbound-traffic correction in hand elsewhere, so whoever lands second covers that paragraph (the probe is a road in the census below; the sentence is the census's `Not this PR` note). Where the code departs from the design note this change was built from, with the reason: the line renders under both metrics, tokens and cost, not in cost mode only, because the view says where its prices came from whenever it is open (the presence rule), and whether it should be cost-only is the one question left open for the fork's owner; a separate `#ra-price` node, present with its text and absent without, never an empty node, in place of a clause appended to the footnote; the age from the kernel's ageS in words through the webview's one age helper (agoWords, bound as raAgo), not raWhen over fetchedAt; no `_async` keyword, because the tests join the price-refresh thread by name and the signature stays literally unchanged; lastError a reason class, not a `Type: message` clip, so nothing from the response or the URL reaches the label; six wordings under defaults where the note wrote four (inflight said apart from unfetched; empty worded two ways by `matched`); the five reason values are the note's; the kernel test module named tests/test_price_feed_off.py, not the note's tests/test_price_feed_switch.py; `built-in defaults` on every surface where the note wrote `baked-in table`; the off line a deliberate step past the catalog's silent off (the requirement that the log and the view say defaults), not a copy error; the webview pins executed through gear.js in ui/webview/analytics-price-source.test.ts and analytics-price-source-states.test.ts, where the note placed source pins in rail-spend.test.ts and marked the raPriceNote extraction optional, because requiring gear.js under node was unverified when the note was written and the build verified it (gear.js's top level is declarations, requires and the export), so rail-spend.test.ts is unchanged and the note's pin that kernel.py emits priceFeed in both _token_analytics and _version_info is executed in tests/test_price_feed_off.py rather than pinned as source from the webview side; the worker stamping fetchedAt with the attempt's `now` (the /analytics handler's clock, the one the TTL stamp uses) where the note wrote int(time.time()) at success, so ageS and the TTL are computed on one clock and the tests drive both with one synthetic time, the difference being the fetch's own duration; and `_price_feed_lock` where the note's risks section said no lock is needed (written for single-key writes), because round 1 made the view read the block as a compound (source with rows and known, reason with matched, the feed line with lastError), so a landing has to be seen whole or not at all. The fork manager's first review round added four more departures, each in the round paragraph below with its reason: the block carries a boolean, `unrecognised`, for a switch value that is set and not off, and never the value, where the design note's status shape had no such key (the block rides the auth-exempt /version); the finiteness check on rates covers the override file's parse as well as the feed's, where the note's population had the file as a read-only input; the census of outbound connections is a derived table with a re-runnable script, scripts/network-inventory.py, where the note's ask listed six roads by hand; and _price_feed_lock also serialises the say-once latches as test-and-sets, where the note's risks section said single-key writes needed no lock. The fork manager's second review round added two more, each with its reason in its round paragraph below: the TTL check and its stamp take _price_feed_lock with the attempt's fields as one transition, where the earlier review had refuted the check-then-stamp race by trial count, because a parked read of the stamp reproduces two requests for one stale cache on both interpreters; and the in-flight mark is a per-flight token cleared under the lock by its own flight, where the status shape had a boolean, because the first of two flights to end cleared the shared boolean for both. The additions the note did not have (the partial share, the override count, `matched`, the feed line carrying the switch or the last failed refresh, a landed or failed result outranking a fetch in flight, inflight worded as a fetch under way) are the second paragraph's, and tests/test_price_feed_vocabulary.py pins the reasons, sources and keys the two sides share.

The first review round by the fork's manager, 2026-09-20, landed on the same branch before its re-review, changed the following. The block describes the table that priced the figures beside it on both /analytics roads because the serve is guarded, not the store: the memo entry carries the merged table its figures were priced with (`prices` in _ANALYTICS_MEMO) and is served only while that is the table now (_price_feed_status(now, memo["prices"]) on the memo road, a rebuild when it returns None), the miss road memoizes only once a block describes its payload, and the one rebuild's table moving again serves the figures with no block (`priceFeed` null, no line in the modal) and memoizes nothing, the disclosed residual; before, the memo road read a fresh status with no table check, so an edit to model-prices.json inside the 15 s memo window paired memoized dollars with a block counting an override that never priced them, and the worker's memo clear covered a feed landing alone. Only `off`, whitespace and case ignored, turns the feed off (the catalog's spelling, unchanged and pinned once in the source); a value that is set and not off leaves the feed on and is now said: one stderr line per kernel life at the first attempt that reads it (the attempt is the event, boot is not), naming the variable and the value it read (repr, clipped to 40 characters), through _price_feed_unrecognised, read per call like its sibling and checked after the off arm and before the TTL check so a refused attempt never reads as unrecognised, and the block's boolean `unrecognised` on the modal's line and /version, never the value, because the block rides the auth-exempt /version and carries no environment text; the choice the manager may re-rule is that split, the log names the value and the block carries the boolean. Every say-once latch (offSaid, unrecognisedSaid, overrideFileSaid, and overrideRowsSaid, a frozenset of the row keys named, replaced under the lock) is a test-and-set under _price_feed_lock through _price_feed_first, with the line written outside the lock because _price_feed_line re-enters it through _price_feed_status; before, offSaid was a check-then-set outside the lock and two builds arriving together wrote the off line twice. A rate that is not a finite number (JSON NaN or Infinity, which json.loads accepts) is rejected at both parses, the feed's (counted in `matched`, never in `rows`, never cached, said as a landing with no usable row) and the override file's (a rejected row); before, such a feed row was cached as a live row and serialized as a bare NaN token the browser could not parse. A row of model-prices.json the kernel cannot read (not an object, or a rate that is not a finite number) is skipped alone and every other row applies, wherever in the file it sits, a file it cannot read or parse as a JSON object (a JSON list included) is ignored whole, a missing file is nothing, and the block and /version carry the class as `overrideFault` (null, `row` or `file`) and the count of skipped rows as `overrideRowsRejected`, said on stderr with the consequence (the second paragraph's lines); both travel on the merged table itself (_PriceTable, the dict _model_prices returns), so the status reports the fault and the count of the very merge it counts overrides from, exact under its lock. The round as first landed had two deltas, and the manager re-ruled both on 2026-09-21: a row the kernel could not read was rejected together with every row after it (one try around the whole loop, where the base had skipped a non-object row silently and kept the rest), rejected because the same bad row placed first voided the user's whole table and placed last voided nothing, a reach that was an artifact of the parse and not a rule, and ruled the shape the unrecognised switch value has: the row alone rejected, every other row kept, the stderr line naming the row and saying the rest applies, the block carrying the fact as a count; and the say-once latch was one across both fault classes, which let the first fault of either class silence the other class's first for the rest of the kernel's life, ruled one latch per class (`overrideFileSaid`; `overrideRowsSaid`, keyed by row so each bad row is named once and a row that goes bad later is named too). _price_feed_off's docstring names the update check's switch by its real name, _update_checks_off, and states the rule in place of a precedent inventory. tests/test_price_feed_census.py pins, by ast over kernel/kernel.py and loading no romp code, that PRICE_FEED_URL is read at the worker's urlopen alone, that _refresh_remote_prices is entered from _model_prices alone, and that _model_prices lets a fetch start from _token_analytics alone (the refresh=False callers being the guard's road and the status's own merge), classify-or-red: a site not in its table fails naming file, function path and line and the two resolutions, and a classified site that is gone fails as a stale row. The one-rebuild bound of _token_analytics (_ANALYTICS_REPRICING) has a test: a table that moves on every read is priced twice at most and served with no block. The three test assertions that read the block out of the build that started the fetch (the list below) read the landing after the join or from a second build inside the TTL, the rule now in the harness's docstring and its helper PriceFeedCase._settled; the control case was the free-threaded red in CI, and the two others were the same defect not yet fired. The minutes branch of the stderr tail's age and three legs of _price_feed_error_class (a resolver herror, an SSLError with no reason token, an errno past a C int) are executed by tests. tests/test_price_feed_floor.py pins the import-time half of the runner's floor on conftest's source (TheFloorsAreOnConftestsSource, an ast walk over tests/conftest.py for the module-level assignment and the autouse fixture's re-assert), for ROMP_PRICE_FEED and ROMP_MODEL_CATALOG under one helper, beside the per-test half it already pinned by execution; the catalog's cases sit beside the feed's there because an import into the catalog's module would load the kernel a second time. docs/reference.md's price feed subsection states the switch's condition as the code has it (only `off`, whitespace and case ignored; any other value, `0`, `false` and `no` included, leaves the feed on, and the kernel says so on stderr naming the value, on the line and in the block as the fact alone) where it read `with the variable set`; says the omitted-rate rule as _model_prices has it (a rate the row omits keeps the table's only for an id the table itself names, zero for any other id, matched by exact id and inheriting nothing), the prose branch, the base resolution unchanged and guarded by APartialOverrideRow; says what an unreadable row or file does; and says the cache keeps the rows it parsed, not matched. SECURITY.md's Network access section no longer says romp makes one outbound request by default; it is written from the table below: `By default the kernel opens one connection of its own to a host other than the model provider` (the price feed, with no credential), then the kernel's other default connections (the catalog refresh and the fast-mode probe, both to the model provider), the programs it runs by default that connect on their own (`git ls-remote` for the release and drift checks, `git ls-remote --heads origin` in a viewed file's checkout, the session and judge CLIs, one `npm install` at a failed boot rebuild), the connections that open once set up, the by-hand install scripts, a sentence that what those programs send is theirs, and the telemetry claim scoped to what the table supports (romp sends no telemetry; session text goes only to the model provider the session or the judge call is billed to and, encrypted end to end, to the push service of a phone you subscribed); the switch paragraph there also states the value rule. The round's tests, classified: of the 52 cases this round added or rewrote (50 Python across tests/test_price_feed_consistency.py, tests/test_price_feed_off.py, tests/test_price_feed_census.py, tests/test_reference_price_feed.py, tests/test_reference_price_feed_rows.py, tests/test_security_price_feed.py and tests/test_price_feed_floor.py, and 2 node in ui/webview/analytics-price-source-states.test.ts), 29 Python cases are red over the archive each names (the head the round reviewed, the ninth commit on the branch, for 28; the PR's base for LiveFeed's non-finite rate case, which is red at both) at the assertion each pins, identically on CPython 3.12.3 and free-threaded 3.14.6; 8 are mutation-proved at this head (TheTailNamesTheShare's minutes case; FailedFetch's TLS-and-resolver case, three legs; APartialOverrideRow, a guard green at both archives; the census module's three site pins, TheFeedHasOneFetchSite's url, refresh-caller and merge-caller cases; the floor module's two source pins), each mutation re-run in a scratch copy of the tree and red there, green at the tree; 12 are controls, guards or self-tests, green and saying so (the census module's anchors guard, its first-statement text twin and TheCensusIsLive's six; the three in-flight members, red as found under the recorder's 50 ms delay on both interpreters and green fixed with and without it; the narrowed override-count case in the reference module, whose moved tie is red in ThePartialRowRule); one, SayOnceLatches' second-latch case, is red before its assertion on the arm that did not exist at the archive, its docstring says so, and its lock proof is the unlocked mutant reading 2 != 1 on both interpreters; and none is left red on a symbol the fix introduced (the key-presence red of TheOverrideFileIsSaid.test_no_file_is_no_fault_and_no_line is an AssertionError at its own assertion, its subject the new key, and its docstring says so). The three in-flight members separately: the control (tests/test_price_feed_consistency.py:299 at that ninth commit) red by timing alone on 3.14t, 3 of 3 in module order, and green on 3.12; LiveFeed's override-row case (tests/test_price_feed_off.py:594) and FailedFetch's failed-refresh case (:482) green by timing in module order on both interpreters; all three red 3 of 3 on both interpreters with the 50 ms worker delay, and green fixed over the archive with and without the delay (36 of 36 member runs) and at the worktree. The two node cases are red over that ninth commit's archive at their first behavioural assertion and green at the tree, 8 of 8, in the view's own node runs. The re-ruling's tests, classified. It replaced one of those 52, TheOverrideFileIsSaid's case that a bad row voided the rows after it; rewrote four more of that class (the non-object row, the non-finite rate, the file class and the no-file case, the last two now asserting the count) and the two AnUnreadableRowOrFileIsSaid cases of tests/test_reference_price_feed_rows.py; and added six to TheOverrideFileIsSaid (the bad row first; the same row last or in the middle, three placements as subtests; two bad rows with a row that goes bad later; a long key clipped; the file class then a row; a row then the file class), one to SayOnceLatches (two builds meeting a skipped row together name it once) and two node cases to ui/webview/analytics-price-source-states.test.ts: 15 cases, 13 Python and 2 node. Every one is red for its stated reason over a git archive of the round's head with the harness fix (the eleventh commit on the branch) with the final test modules copied in, identically on CPython 3.12.3 and free-threaded CPython 3.14.6 with the GIL read off in-process: the three Python classes selected run 16 failed and 2 passed there (13 cases, the placement case's three subtests counted separately; the 2 passed are the older SayOnceLatches cases), the node file 8 pass and 2 fail. At the assertion each pins: the bad row first, `1e-05 != 1.2e-05 : a good row applies` (the archive's one try around the loop voided both good rows); the placements, last `None != 1 : the block counts the skipped row` (its table is green there by accident, a last row voiding nothing, and the archive has no overrideRowsRejected key, as its docstring says), middle `3e-06 != 4e-06 : and so does the other good row, wherever the bad row sits`, first `1e-05 != 1.2e-05 : a good row applies`, then `0 != 3 : three placements ran`; two bad rows, `1e-05 != 1.2e-05 : the good row between two bad ones applies`; the non-object row, `Tuples differ: (1.2e-05, 3e-06) != (1.2e-05, 4e-06) : both object rows apply`; the non-finite rate, `Tuples differ: (0, None) != (0, 1)`; the file class, `None != 0 : no row was read, so none was skipped: the count is 0`; the no-file case, `'overrideRowsRejected' not found in {...} : so is the count`, an AssertionError at its own assertion with the new key as its subject; the clipped key, `0 != 1` at the row line's count, the message quoting the archive's log with its line that names no row; the two class-latch cases, each at the line count of the class said second, `0 != 1 : the second class's first fault is said, on its own latch:` for the file class then a row and `0 != 1 : the file class's first fault is said, on its own latch:` for the reverse (the first class's check passes at both heads, the file line's text unchanged and the row check counting a phrase both heads' row lines carry, so each order is red at the class the archive's one latch silenced); SayOnceLatches' skipped-row case, `False is not true : the gate saw the first read of the latch` (the archive never reads the key), its lock proof the set latch's test-and-set moved outside the lock in a scratch copy of the tree, red `2 != 1 : one line per kernel life per row, whatever arrives together:` on both interpreters and green at the tree; and both reference cases, `docs/reference.md (The price feed) does not carry 'A row the kernel cannot read (not an object, or a rate that is not a finite number) is skipped and every other row applies, wherever in the file the bad row sits'`. At the tree (the worktree at that head with the re-ruling's edits): the seven price feed modules (tests/test_price_feed_off.py 46 cases in ten classes, tests/test_price_feed_consistency.py 17, tests/test_price_feed_census.py 11, tests/test_price_feed_floor.py 4, tests/test_price_feed_vocabulary.py 9, tests/test_reference_price_feed.py 15, tests/test_reference_price_feed_rows.py 6) run 108 passed and 29 subtests passed on each interpreter, the two harness modules 63 passed and 25 subtests in 5 of 5 runs each; under node, the four webview files that name raPriceNote: the states test 10 of 10, analytics-price-source 9 of 9, gear 20 of 20, rail-spend 18 of 18. **The in-flight block reads this round fixed**, a population derived by execution over an archive of the reviewed head on CPython 3.12.3 and free-threaded 3.14.6 before any fix, every read of the block in the two modules walked by ast; three members, at the archive's line numbers:
1. tests/test_price_feed_consistency.py APartialLandingIsSaid.test_control_a_landing_whose_signed_rows_all_parse_says_nothing, line 299: red-by-timing. It read (rows, matched) from the payload of the build that started the fetch and expected the landing (1, 1); with no delay added, green 16 of 16 on 3.12 and red 12 of 16 on 3.14t (3 of 3 in module order, 9 of 13 alone) as `Tuples differ: (0, 0) != (1, 1)`; a 50 ms sleep in the recorder before the worker answers reds it 3 of 3 on both interpreters with the same message.
2. tests/test_price_feed_off.py FailedFetch.test_a_failed_refresh_after_a_landed_fetch_says_the_cached_rows_still_serve, line 482: green-by-timing. It read lastError from the payload of the build that started the failing re-attempt; green 16 of 16 with no delay on each interpreter; the 50 ms delay reds it 3 of 3 on both as `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'startswith'`.
3. tests/test_price_feed_off.py LiveFeed.test_an_override_row_is_counted_in_the_status_and_the_line, line 594: red-by-timing. It read (source, rows, overrides) from the payload of the build at NOW + 1 that started the case's first fetch (the earlier build ran under the switch and stamped nothing); green 16 of 16 on 3.12, on 3.14t green 3 of 3 in module order and red 9 of 13 alone as `Tuples differ: ('defaults', 0, 1) != ('feed', 1, 1)`; the 50 ms delay reds it 3 of 3 on both interpreters.
Ruled not members: the sibling reads at tests/test_price_feed_off.py line 481 and tests/test_price_feed_consistency.py line 315, on the same fetch-starting builds, read the earlier joined landing's fields (source, rows, fetchedAt, ageS, known), which a failing re-attempt cannot change, and are green under the delay 3 of 3 on both interpreters; every other block read in the two modules is from a second build inside the TTL after the join, a joined refresh, the status function after the join, a build under the switch, or a gated worker whose flight is the assertion's subject, all green under the delay in 12 of 12 module runs. Fixed (the control's counts from a second build inside the TTL after the join, the failed refresh's lastError from the status after the join, the override case from a second build at the same clock after the join), all three pass in every run over the archive with and without the delay and at the tree on both interpreters. The rule, now in the harness's docstring: any assertion about a landing or a failure reads it after the join or from a second build inside the TTL, never from the payload of the build that started the fetch.

The second review round by the fork's manager, 2026-09-21, over the whole change at the head the first round's fixes and the harness fix left, ruled 47 findings (3 high, 24 medium, 18 low, two refuted and not refiled) and changed the following, one cluster at a time. The instrument: the census is run by the suite and refuses loudly (the paragraph and the table below), where the round found it run by nothing, unable to fail on a short result (the kernel's main file moved one directory down in a scratch copy took it from 266 sites and 30 roads to 165 and 16 with exit 0 and 0 unclassified), filing a shell or node child and every browser fetch local by spelling, and folding a second site in a rowed function silently; the census's row key stays file plus enclosing function with a committed count per key, so a second site in a rowed function changes its key's count and fails the run naming the key and both counts (a line in the key would go stale on every edit above a site in kernel/kernel.py and be regenerated by habit, which accepts a new site as readily as a moved one). The override row's rate: a rate that is present and not a JSON number (null, a string, a numeric string too, a list, an object, false or true) is rejected by type into the per-row reject path, classed `row`, counted in `overrideRowsRejected` and named once on stderr, never coerced; until this round `float(x or 0)` priced such a row at zero per token, or at a dollar a token for `true`, while the block read a clean override and stderr said nothing; only an absent key inherits the table's rate; the row's stderr line names the three classes (not an object, a rate that is not a number, or a rate that is not finite) and docs/reference.md states the predicate beside the two it had (the string's arm is re-ruled in the B re-ruling, below: a quoted plain decimal is read as that number). The gate: the TTL check (the read of `_price_cache["t"]`) and its stamp take `_price_feed_lock` with the attempt's fields as one transition, so exactly one caller per TTL window proceeds and one request leaves for one stale cache; before, two cost-view builds arriving together each started a worker, two requests and two fetch lines, reproduced at the head and at the base on both interpreters by parking the first thread's read of the stamp (the earlier review's refutation of the same race, one worker in 4000 trials, was a timing observation and not a proof). The in-flight mark is a per-flight token, the attempt counter `flights` incremented under the lock, cleared under the lock only by its own flight, so the first of two flights to end leaves a later flight's mark standing; before, a shared boolean the worker's finally cleared outside the lock for every flight; the status reads the mark's truthiness, so the block's keys are unchanged. The whole-file clause: the modal's price line reads `overrideFault` and words the file class as `; model-prices.json could not be read as a JSON object and was ignored (none of it applies)` in the skipped-rows slot on both branches (the second paragraph), where a file discarded whole rendered the clean line; the age in the line comes from the webview's one age helper, agoWords, bound as raAgo (now under 45 seconds, then rounded minutes, rounded hours under a day, then days), the local copy with its own thresholds and no day arm deleted, and the kernel's stderr tail gains a day arm at 24 hours so the log and the modal agree on the unit. The section: SECURITY.md's Network access section is derived from the census's `--table` output sentence by sentence and every exhaustive quantifier the table cannot support is gone: the closing sentence names three destinations for session text (the model provider billed; a machine you attached, over your own ssh tunnels; a subscribed phone's push service, encrypted end to end) in place of an `only` over two; the set-up frame is worded to the actor, you or a session you are running, with the watch predicate as its own item (the route, the shell, the cadence floor and default, the bound, the boot re-arm); the browser's pictures road sits in the by-default group with the gear's shipped host list; the trigger is keyed on the last fetch attempt, stamped before the fetch runs, so a failed or empty fetch holds the six hours like a landed one and the first open after a start always fetches; bootstrap.pypa.io and ROMP_NO_GET_PIP are named; the release and drift checks carry their cadences and the drift verdict's three disjuncts; the derivation passage names the script, the committed counts, what the run refuses and the four classes the scan cannot derive, without counts, since the section is long-lived and this entry and the counts file carry the figures; and tests/test_security_price_feed.py TheSectionIsTheTables holds the section's road list equal to `--table` by execution, each sentence pinned to the code fact behind it. docs/reference.md says where each clause of the modal's line sits (the unrecognised clause after the source and before the override count, the count before the skipped-rows clause, only the last slot ending the line, pinned to raPriceNote's push order by index), states the rate predicate, conditions the stderr sentence on the cost view's road (the spend ceiling's check and /version price with the same file and write no line; the block carries the class whether or not the view was opened; pinned by ast on the refresh guard around both fault lines), and quotes the whole-file clause. The pins: the two OneSnapshot cases wait for the thread that asks for `_price_feed_lock` while the other holds it (a stand-in over the lock records the ask and the owner) in place of a fixed second, and prove themselves by mutation, the lock taken out of the status's snapshot or out of the landing failing them ten runs of ten on CPython 3.12 and free-threaded 3.14 with the tree green ten of ten; the floor pin is one case that pops the switch and runs conftest's own fixture function, so the re-assert executes in whatever worker the case lands in; the vocabulary pin checks the block's keys both ways, with the two keys the view leaves unworded (attemptedAt and fetchedAt) declared with their reasons, and is red over the head's archive naming overrideFault; the wiring pin executes raRender over the DOM stand-in and anchors the call's place on the call alone, so the footnote's copy can change without touching it; and the dead `rows` field of the status dict (written under the lock by every landing, read by nothing) is gone, the dict literal's keys held equal to the harness's reset keys. The population of kernel-launching harnesses that copy tests/conftest.py's network floors, derived by reading every ROMP_MODEL_CATALOG spelling outside the kernel, has three members without the price feed's floor: tools/ui-bench.mjs's bench kernel gains ROMP_PRICE_FEED=off with tests/ui-bench.test.mjs asserting it; tools/perf-bench.py (with tests/test_perf_bench.py asserting it) and ui/webview/file-comments.test.ts are the other two and take the same floor in this round. The record: the census paragraph and the table below are dated to the branch head in words and the table is the script's own output, held to it by execution; the browser row lost its two Copy image clauses because the script files that fetch local (it reads the kernel's own file URL), which is how a hand-written cell was told apart from a derived one; the Recorded, not fixed list is labelled from (a) (no item was dropped: the commit that added it recorded two follow-ons and labelled them (b) and (c)). The round's tests, classified: 55 of 67 cases this round added or rewrote across tests/test_price_feed_census.py, tests/test_price_feed_off.py, tests/test_price_feed_consistency.py, tests/test_price_feed_floor.py, tests/test_price_feed_vocabulary.py, tests/test_security_price_feed.py, tests/test_reference_price_feed.py, tests/test_reference_price_feed_rows.py, tests/ui-bench.test.mjs, ui/webview/analytics-price-source-states.test.ts, ui/webview/analytics-price-source.test.ts and ui/webview/gear.test.ts are red over the archive each names (the reviewed head's for behaviour the change introduced; the base's for the coercion, the check-then-stamp and the merge) at the assertion it pins, identically on CPython 3.12.3 and free-threaded 3.14.6 for the Python cases and under node for the rest, 6 are mutation-proved at this head, 5 are controls or guards, 1 is red at an anchor assertion whose subject is the new API (the census's ROADS literal, named as such), and none is left red on a symbol the fix introduced: the one error-shaped red, the flight token's AttributeError over the base archive, is at an archive that is not that case's archive of record, and at the reviewed head's archive it is red at its assertion. The where: line names every changed path (29 at this tree, the committed counts file, the two benches and their tests, and the dist ratchet's tree-copier allowlist naming the census module among them) and the 20 top-level kernel/kernel.py definitions whose source differs from the base's, checked by ast over the base's file and the tree's (18 after round 2; the B re-ruling adds _PRICE_RATE_STRING and _price_rate_value).

What the switch stops is one request, the price feed's; every other request romp makes goes out as before. The table below is the census script's render_table output over the tree at the branch head. tests/test_price_feed_census.py TheTableIsTheLedgers holds the block between its two marker lines equal to that output by execution, composed in its own process from one scan of the tree, so no row in it is written by hand; the script's `--table` road (`python3 scripts/network-inventory.py --table`) is held to print render_table's output by runs over two tiny roots, not over the tree. The census is run by the suite, on every test run and so in CI's Python job on every pull request and push to main: tests/test_price_feed_census.py derives the tree once in its own process (the script's scan, figures, problems, listing and table composed in main's order from one scan, without calling main), runs its mutations over a copy of the scanned scope, alone or joined into shared runs (a site one directory down, a shell as argv[0], an absolute URL in a browser fetch, an import the census does not know, a second site inside a function that already has a row, a row naming no site, The script exits 1, naming the reason, on a site with no road, a row naming no site, no sites at all, a missing declared root, a Python file that does not parse, an import it does not know (a Python module outside its allow-list, whether imported or named to importlib.import_module or __import__, a module named there at run time, or a package the browser and editor code imports that the census does not know, KNOWN_JS_IMPORTS; the moved-file case the second round reproduced by hand is a test case now, red at the archive and green at the tree, where the run exits 1 naming the stale rows, the changed per-key count and the moved file's unclassified sites. Its walk is recursive over kernel/, cli/, postal/, bin/, hooks/, ui/ and vscode-extension/src (test files, __pycache__, node_modules and symlinks skipped; the browser and editor trees read for their own kinds, so a Python fixture under ui/ is skipped by kind, while a dotted file under the Python roots outside the five extensions is read for its shebang, so a .bash or .zsh hook is scanned where until the fourth round it was skipped by kind), plus bootstrap.sh, install.sh, vscode-extension/install.sh and tools/file-comments-host.mjs, the one program the kernel starts from tools/ (the earlier walk was flat and took hooks/ as shell files only; the two files it never opened, a node hook and a CRLF fixture, have no site at this head). vendor/track-changents is runtime code, reached by relative imports from walked files (the dashboard's ui sources, a session hook and the file-comments host) and by install.sh's links into ~/.claude, and it is not walked. A load written there is no site and no line, and the import gate does not read its imports. The pages the kernel serves and its service worker's script, from its own string constants (the dashboard shell, the seven pane pages, the token login page, the too-large page and /sw.js, with the shim, the timeline boot and the shell scripts they inline), are read from kernel.py's syntax tree and scanned as browser text keyed kernel/kernel.py plus tool, with the DOM loads counted. A string constant is scanned whole; a bytes constant whose every byte is ASCII is scanned the same way, each byte its own character, and one holding a byte past ASCII is refused by name (below); and a name bound once, by one top-level plain assignment, to a call of `compile` handed a string or bytes constant (and at most a flags argument that is an int constant, an attribute of that name, or their `|`) on a name the module binds to the standard library's `re` by binding is read through its `.search`, `.match`, `.fullmatch` and `.findall`, and through its `.sub` where the replacement is a string or bytes constant holding no backslash, which change nothing, so such a call's arguments are the page text and the compiled name holds none of its own; a `.sub` with any other replacement (one holding a backslash, which `re` may expand as a template escape or a group reference into text the replacement does not spell, a name, a callable, a starred argument or a `**`) is refused by name, since the census does not model template expansion; the proof by binding is the digest leaf's (below), with its limit, so a module object stored in sys.modules under that name before the import binds it, or a function of the module replaced by an attribute store, is not seen. The census reads a page only where the bytes a browser decodes are the text it scans, which it reads as UTF-8, so it refuses by name, as a page it cannot read: a bytes constant holding a byte past ASCII (a byte order mark, whole or split across constants, among them); page text holding U+FEFF, refused on the safe side because a leading U+FEFF is a byte order mark the page's UTF-8 bytes carry; page text holding a NUL, by which a browser may read interleaved NULs as UTF-16; page text declaring a charset other than utf-8 (a `charset=` in any case, as a meta element's charset attribute and a content attribute's parameter spell it, or an XML declaration's `encoding=`, followed by any label but utf-8) in a string or bytes constant, an f-string's literal part or the joined text of a join of string constants; a script-running content type whose parameters name a charset other than utf-8; and a page's read of a walked browser-text file whose text, as the walk decoded it, holds U+FFFD, U+FEFF or a NUL, or declares such a charset. A declaration split across texts the census reads apart (any part but a string constant among its pieces, between two literals or as one half: a name's text, a call, a conditional expression or a bytes constant, which the census reads each on its own) is read as each text spells it. The routes are derived from the calls of `_send` the scan reads (spelled `_send(...)` or `<x>._send(...)`; a call through a name computed at run time is not read) and every Content-Type header written outside `_send`, in every scanned Python file. A `_send` call's content type is read through the definition it reaches, the one def or async def statement that binds `_send` in its file, direct in the call's own class body (a call through self: a call on a name that is self by binding, the first positional parameter of the method the call stands in, however it is spelled, never the spelling self) or in the module (a bare call): the kernel's Handler._send writes its `ctype` parameter, so the call's third argument or its `ctype=` keyword; the postal bus's writes application/json; the session host's and its transport's write a frame to a Unix socket and answer no HTTP request (FRAME_WRITERS), and any other definition that writes no Content-Type fails the run. So does each `_send` call in a file that binds `_send` more than once outside function bodies (the module and every class body counted together, a class body a function body defines included, since a class body is no function body, in any binding form but a comprehension's target, which binds only in its comprehension) or other than by one def statement direct in a class body or the module, or where a function or a class body binds it under a `global` declaration, rebinding the module's name at run time, each bare call that reaches a `_send` bound inside a function, a lambda, a comprehension or a class body around it, in any form (a parameter, a nested def, a loop target, a lambda's parameter and a comprehension's target among them; the line names the scope and the binding), each call that reaches no definition the census reads, the line saying why (a `_send` the call's own class body does not bind, for a call through self: inherited or set at run time; a call on a name in a class body that is not self by binding, the line naming the condition it fails (the call outside the body of a method defined directly in that class body, or in that method's header, which runs in the class body; the name not the method's first positional parameter; a lambda or a comprehension around the call binding the name; the method binding the name again in any form, or a scope nested in it rebinding it under a nonlocal declaration; a decorator on the method other than a name, or a call of a name, that the module binds once by a def statement; the class body binding the method's name other than by that one def statement, or declaring it global or nonlocal; the file storing, deleting or naming to a setter an attribute of the method's name on any object); one reached in a class body through a receiver that is no name (an object other than self), or through an attribute outside any class body; a `_send` no scope the bare call looks it up in binds, the module included; and, in a file that names `_send` nowhere but as the called name of those calls, so binds it nowhere at all, a definition the file does not hold), each call to a definition carrying any decorator, whose parameters the census does not read, and each bare call in a module that holds a star import. An override of `_send` in a subclass that another file defines is not read: a call through self is typed through its own class body's definition (where that file binds the page's module by an import statement, the page's file fails the run, below). Nor is what a decorator the census accepts on the method (a name, or a call of a name, that the module binds once by a def statement) does with self, since that def may hand the method another object in the instance's place (its witness: a method whose decorator, a def of the file, calls it with an object whose `_send` serves the page as text/html), or a method replaced on its class at run time through a name no code spells, a setter whose name argument does not fold among them (its witness: a method a setattr replaces, its name the return of a call). The type is read through a module name no code writes after binding it (one plain single-name assignment binds it as a top-level statement, nothing else at module level binds it, a walrus in a def's or a class's header included, the module holds no star import, and nothing in the file writes that name, in any scope: a subscript store or delete, a call `<name>.<method>(` of a method _MUTATORS or _DUNDER_MUTATORS lists, a call of such a method on a type _CONTAINER_TYPES lists with the name as its first argument (`dict.update(<name>, ...)`), a binding in a function that declares the name `global` (as the target of an assignment, an augmented assignment, a loop, a comprehension, a with or a walrus, or by an import, a def or class statement or an except clause) or a module-level augmented assignment), through a local whose every binding is read (a walrus in a nested def's, class's or lambda's header is a binding it does not read) and through a dict literal's values (refused before its type is read, below: a call inside a lambda's body, a Content-Type write inside one that is no `_send` definition's own write, and a call whose definition binds the parameter one of its own writes names other than as that parameter); a type holding a CR or LF is refused by name before it is compared (its header line ends there, and what follows is another header or the body, which the census does not read); the part before any `;`, stripped and lower-cased, is compared with the types a browser runs script from (SCRIPT_TYPES: text/html; the XML types text/xml, application/xml, text/xsl and any type with a `+xml` suffix, image/svg+xml and application/xhtml+xml among them; and text/javascript under each name a browser takes for JavaScript, application/javascript among them). A script-running route's page body is the call's second positional argument, read only when the call passes it positionally, with no starred argument before it and no `**`, and the definition's one output is its one `self.wfile.write(<its second positional parameter>)`, with a signature of positional parameters, none positional-only, with None defaults and no annotation, no node in its body of a kind the kernel's Handler._send holds none of, and every statement of its own body one of that definition's statement shapes, which the census lists from it (_SEND_SHAPES): a statement's place in the order, its kind, each operator, each attribute's name, the codec's name by its value, and each other operand by its class: self (the method's first parameter); the page (its second positional parameter); another parameter; a name a loop's target binds, read in that loop; a flag, a text name and the header parameter, each a name a preamble role below binds, a flag also where it is an if's whole test; any other name a statement stores that is no parameter, a local (a loop's target among them); a constant by its type (a string, a bool, an int, None); a builtin by its name (str, len, isinstance and getattr in the shapes below), where neither the definition nor the module binds the name, nothing rebinds it (no function binds it under a `global` declaration, no module-level statement writes it, and the file does none of the writes listed below that rebind every builtin) and the module holds no star import; a module constant (a name one top-level plain assignment binds and nothing else at module level, that nothing rebinds and no local or parameter of the definition names, in a module with no star import that writes no name of its module namespace through a computed name and may not rewrite it at run time) whose bound text holds no CR or LF as the census reads it (below); an attribute on self other than a header method or wfile, one class whatever its name; and page text, which the served pass reads; any other name (a parameter a statement stores outside those roles among them) stands in no listed shape. The shapes, each matched by at most as many statements as the kernel's definition holds in it (a constant header by four, every other shape by one), `<str>` a string constant and every if and loop with no else: before the end_headers, the stamp `<page> = <page text>`; a flag `<flag> = <a bool constant>`; the injection's if, `if getattr(self, <str>, <a bool constant>) and isinstance(<page>, str) and <parameter>.startswith(<str>) and <str> in <page>:` whose body is `<text name> = <page text>`, then `if getattr(self, <str>, None):` holding `<text name> = <page text>` and `<flag> = <a bool constant>`, then `<page> = <page>.replace(<str>, <page text>, <an int constant>)`; the flag's if, `if <flag>: <header parameter> = <str>`; the codec, `<page> = <page>.encode("utf-8") if isinstance(<page>, str) else <page>`; the response line, `self.send_response(<parameter>)`; a header from a parameter, `self.send_header(<str>, <parameter>)`; the length header, `self.send_header(<str>, str(len(<page>)))`; a constant header, `self.send_header(<str>, <str>)`; a constant header under a getattr test, `if getattr(self, <str>, <a bool constant>): self.send_header(<str>, <str>)`; the headers loop, `for <target>, <target> in (<parameter> or {}).items(): self.send_header(<target>, <target>)`, each `<target>` a name the loop's target binds; a header under its parameter's test, `if <parameter>: self.send_header(<str>, <parameter>)`; a formatted header under a getattr test, `if getattr(self, <str>, None): self.send_header(<str>, <str> % (<module constant>, <attribute on self>))`; and an attribute header under a getattr test, `if getattr(self, <str>, None):` holding `self.send_header(<str>, <attribute on self>)` then `self.send_header(<str>, <str>)`; directly before the write, the end_headers, `self.end_headers()`; and last, the write, `self.wfile.write(<page>)`. The page text is what the statements that build the page put there before the codec, each read for the text it puts there: a stamp's `F(<the definition's own parameters, positional, none starred and no keyword>)`, F a bare name the served pass follows only where it proves a module function a plain def and reads its returns; an injection's value; and the value of a text name, a name no parameter, the page or self names, read where an injection's value reads it. A flag is a name no parameter, the page or self names, assigned a bool constant and read only as an if's test, any other assignment of it refused; the header parameter, the parameter the flag's if assigns, is a non-body header write, neither the page nor self, its string constant holding no CR or LF. Any other script-running call fails the run by name, its reason naming the road (a second write, a write through an alias, a print to a stream; a node of a kind the shapes hold none of, by its kind and line; any other statement by its kind and line and the shape it lacks, named among those the census lists for its kind at its place, or by the order it stands outside; a statement in a listed shape past its count, by that shape and its count; and in a statement of a listed shape, a builtin or a module constant whose proof fails, by that proof), among them a keyword body, a starred or `**` call, a definition whose one write is of another parameter, a local or an expression, or that writes nothing, a method's definition that binds self again, in any form (a lambda's or a nested def's parameter among them), or declares it global, any other read of an attribute named `write`, `writelines`, `send`, `sendall`, `sendfile` or `sendmsg`, called or not, a string constant equal to one of those names, a reference to the write's receiver other than as its receiver, and in the definition a statement after the end_headers (which writes the header buffer to the stream, so a header call after it would reach the body), a second end_headers or none, any other rebinding of the page parameter, another codec name or a second argument to `.encode` (either can name a codec or an error handler the file registers at run time), a store or delete of an attribute or a subscript, a read of a name the module binds other than a module constant in a formatted header's tuple or in page text, a module constant there whose bound text holds a CR or LF or reads a name the census does not follow by binding (by name), a string constant holding a CR or LF in a header call (by name), a call in no listed shape, and a nested def, class or lambda. The reader governs the definition's own text, and code the definition runs from outside that text is not read: a header value is not scanned (a module constant the definition uses as one is read only for a CR or LF: each string or bytes constant in its value and in the value of each module constant that value names, followed by binding (a method call's receiver, as `T` in `T.lower()`, among the names followed), any other name there refusing it, save a bare name a call calls (`f` in `f(...)`); and so is each argument a `_send` call hands its definition but the page body and the content type (read above), positional, starred or keyword, a `**` among them: each string or bytes constant in it, a dict literal's keys and values among them, and each name in it that one top-level plain assignment binds and no function scope around the call binds, read through the module constants its value names, any other name there not read, one holding a CR or LF refusing the call by name before it is typed; a CR or LF the value computes at run time, a call's return or a number formatted as a character, is not read, nor is a header value held anywhere else, a local, a parameter, an attribute, a call's return or an item, its witnesses a CR LF from chr and one from a `%c` of an int, and a header value held in a local dict and a CR LF a call of chr computes at the call), and a response that a Content-Type in its headers argument, passed or defaulted, makes a page is outside the served pass, the call being typed by its content-type argument; nor is code the definition runs through an object it is handed (a parameter's methods, its mapping's items, its __str__), code behind a name the definition calls or reads on self (a header method, a property or `__getattr__`, however the class, a base or other code defines or replaces it) and any stream that code writes, or a `_send` replaced at run time through a name no code spells (setattr or a class `__dict__` with a computed name, a metaclass namespace key, a base's `__init_subclass__`). The census does not see a module namespace rewritten at run time by code outside the forms listed below that rebind every name: a listed name reached any other way or a name the list does not hold (through `__self__` of a builtin, a container or a copy of a namespace mapping other than the file's own module's or the builtins' (one of those used other than in a key position the census reads is a run-time form, below), a module's own `__setattr__` or `__delattr__` method, a listed name, attrgetter or methodcaller imported from a module other than its own, a name built at run time, gc or ctypes among them, and through a module reached by a tuple or list unpacking, an inline walrus, `sys.modules.__getitem__`, a for-loop target, a parameter default or a starred argument) is outside the list and not seen, and a module name or a builtin so rewritten is read as the file's text binds it; nor does it see a scalar local or parameter a function rebinds at run time through its own frame (the frame's locals, which Python 3.13 and later write through to the function, reached by `sys._getframe()`, `inspect.currentframe()` or `inspect.getargvalues()` among other calls): a page's text, and a content type that is a string constant or a name bound once to one, so rebound the census reads as the function's text binds it, a stated limit on Python 3.13 and later (its witnesses: a handler that binds its content type to `application/json` and one that binds its page to `<p>ok</p>`, each rebinding that local through its frame, so that those versions serve a fetch as a page). A container a function binds and reads whole, changed through that same frame's local mapping, reached or run as code in the function's own body (one of the seven bare names `locals`, `vars`, `exec`, `eval`, `_getframe`, `currentframe`, `getargvalues`, one of the four attributes `_getframe`, `currentframe`, `getargvalues` or `f_locals` spelled as an attribute on any receiver, or a name that a statement of the module's or the function's own body, outside every def, class or lambda statement it nests (header and body alike), binds to one of those primitives, its bound value the primitive's own bare name or attribute, by a plain or annotated assignment, a walrus, an unpacking of a list or tuple literal into a target list of as many elements (each plain name there bound to the value at its place, beside a starred or nested target too), or a `from ... import` of one of the seven names, and any name such a statement binds to a name so bound, resolved among those statements), is refused by name on every version, since the change reaches the real object; the check reads the spelling anywhere in the function, called or not, so a name merely spelled like a primitive is refused too, its reason naming the spelling found, not a frame reached; a change to those locals at run time through a frame object, exec or eval reached in any other spelling (among them: the name `locals` or `vars` as an attribute on a receiver; exec or eval reached as an attribute; `f_locals` as a bare name; one of the four attributes named by a string, as `getattr(frame, "f_locals")`; and any binding of a name to a primitive that the resolution above does not read, among them a plain name among the targets of an unpacking whose targets are not as many as its values, a name a nested unpacking target binds, an alias whose bound value is any other expression (an if-expression, a call), a module-level for loop's target, a match statement's capture, a with statement's target (bound to what `__enter__` returns), and a binding in the header or the body of a def, class or lambda statement) is the stated precondition, outside what the census reads, and passes silently (its witnesses, one for each road named: `locals` reached as an attribute on a receiver; `builtins.exec`; `builtins.eval`; `f_locals` called as a bare name; `getattr` handed a frame from `inspect.stack()` and the string `f_locals`; a plain name beside a starred target that takes two values, and a name in a nested target; a module alias bound to an if-expression and one bound to a call's return; a walrus in a module-level def's default, a `from ... import` in a def nested in the function, an assignment inside a module-level def's body, an assignment in a module-level class's body, read as the class's attribute, a walrus in a module-level lambda's default, a module-level for loop's target over a tuple holding `locals`, a module-level match statement's capture of `locals`, and a module-level with statement's target bound to `locals` through `contextlib.nullcontext`), while a name a nested scope binds to a primitive by an assignment carries the spelling in the function's subtree and refuses; code the function runs that the census does not read (a helper it calls as a statement, a method on self, a context manager it enters), reaching such a container to change it by a road other than a hand-off, among them the caller's frame and the garbage collector's heap (gc.get_objects or gc.get_referrers, with no frame, no hand-off and no occurrence of the container's name), is a stated limit instead, the census reading no such code's body for its reach as the call limit reads none for what a callee does with a container it is handed (its witnesses, one for each road named: a module helper the handler calls as a statement that appends a fetch to the handler's container through its caller's frame, and one that finds that container among gc.get_objects() by its content and appends a fetch to it); a content type the census reads other than as a string constant or a name bound once to one is refused by name too, on every version, so no frame can rewrite an intermediate the census had read. starred argument, a call of int or float bound other than to the builtin, a method whose return the census does not compute, called on a receiver the pass reads whose text it is not drawn from, or on the builtin among them) or is one of the seven builtins' names called unbound, at any call on a page expression's path, a join, format, format_map or replace called on the name str, bytes or bytearray, a `.replace`, `.format`, `.join`, `.format_map` or `.encode` in a shape the census does not compute, a `%`, `.format` or `.format_map` on a string constant that is not expanded, a join whose folded text may run past a million characters, `__file__` in a file where a statement binds it, any other receiver or container, any other callee (a module constant, a local, a class, a subscript, a call and a lambda among them), any other bare module name (one bound other than by one assignment, one bound by an annotated, unpacking or chained assignment, one annotated at module
